Amazon’s New Kindle Paperwhite Is Here. Here’s What’s Different From the Last Version.

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From slimmer designs to updated displays and charging features, here's what's changed with Amazon's latest e-readers.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

Amazon has refreshed its Kindle lineup for 2026, introducing new versions of the popular Kindle Paperwhite and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition. Both e-readers have notable design updates, but if you already own a previous model, you might wonder whether it's worth upgrading.

The short answer? It depends on which Kindle you're using. Compared to the 2024 models, the latest Paperwhites offer a sleeker design, updated display technology, and some welcome improvements to everyday usability. The Signature Edition, in particular, gets a few extras that make it feel more like a premium device. Here's what's changed and what's stayed the same.

Amazon Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition

The most noticeable upgrade is the design and appearance. Amazon has made the standard Paperwhite thinner and slightly lighter, reducing its thickness from 7.8 millimeters to 6.8 millimeters. It also weighs approximately 204 grams, compared to the previous model's 211 grams. That might not sound like much, but a slimmer e-reader is always welcome when you're holding it for hours or slipping it into a bag.

The redesigned display construction also creates a more seamless, edge-to-edge look. Amazon uses a new display stack with an oxide thin-film transistor backplane, designed to deliver excellent contrast, deeper blacks, and crisp text. The screen is still 7 inches with a 300-ppi resolution, so don't expect a dramatic jump in sharpness over the 2024 model.

Another small but genuinely useful change is the power button's placement. Instead of sitting along the bottom edge, where it was easy to press accidentally, it's now positioned on the upper-right side.

And if battery life is your biggest concern, there's good news: Amazon still promises up to 12 weeks on a single charge, just like the previous generation. The new model also maintains its IPX8 waterproof rating and adjustable warm lighting.

The Signature Edition receives the same thinner design and updated screen construction, but Amazon has made a few additional changes to distinguish it from the standard Paperwhite.

For starters, it now has an aluminum back instead of the previous model's plastic construction, giving it a more premium look and feel. That upgrade comes with a trade-off, though: At approximately 229 grams, the new Signature Edition is actually a little heavier than its 214-gram predecessor.

The new model also introduces pogo-pin connectors for charging and accessories. These let the Kindle connect to Amazon's new charging stand and a magnetic Page-Turn Cover with physical buttons, which could appeal to readers who miss the page-turn buttons on older e-readers.

One important difference is that the 2024 Signature Edition supported conventional Qi wireless charging, while the 2026 model switches to the new contact-based pogo-pin system.

Amazon also highlights a double-tap feature that lets you advance pages by tapping the side or back of the device, making one-handed reading a little easier.

Otherwise, you'll still get 32GB of storage, an auto-adjusting front light, and up to 12 weeks of battery life. Both generations are waterproof, too.

If you're shopping for a new Kindle rather than replacing an older one, the decision mostly comes down to whether the Signature Edition's premium features are worth an additional $50.

The standard Paperwhite starts at $199.99 and includes 16GB of storage, adjustable warm lighting, and a 7-inch glare-free display.

For $249.99, the Signature Edition doubles the storage to 32GB, adds an aluminum back and automatic brightness adjustment, and supports the new pogo-pin charging accessories. It's also available in Seaglass Green, whereas the regular Paperwhite comes in Graphite.

Both deliver essentially the same core reading experience, so casual readers probably won't miss much by sticking with the less expensive version.

If you already own a 2024 Kindle Paperwhite or Signature Edition and it's working perfectly fine, you probably don't need to replace it. You're still getting the same screen size and resolution, excellent battery life, waterproofing, and access to Amazon's massive Kindle library.

However, if you're upgrading from an older 2021 Paperwhite—or you're shopping for your first e-reader—the 2026 models are worth considering. The thinner design, improved screen construction, and updated controls make the latest lineup more appealing.

For most readers, the regular Paperwhite will be more than enough. But if you read every day, want the premium aluminum design, or like the idea of a case with physical page-turn buttons, the Signature Edition is a worthwhile step up.

Shop the New Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition

XRP Flashes 3-Fold Recovery Setup On Oversold Reading

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Flashes 3-Fold Recovery Setup On Oversold Reading

Scattered bullish signals are crowned by the ultra-low RSI: a mark of a bounce back in 70% of cases studied.

Bitrue’s Research Institute just examined the XRP versus USDT trading pair from January 2023 through November 8, 2026, spotting a familiar oversold position. Trading 55.2% below its yearly peak of $2.58, XRP’s price setup now flashes ‘24’ on the Relative Strength Index (RSI).

That’s a deeply-oversold condition, meaning that XRP’s price is, in theory, under-valued against the broader market. On the 4-hour XRP price charts, XRP’s currently working band is between $1.03 to $1.18, the research notes. The altcoin’s also trading below the midline Bollinger Band (BOLL) of $1.14.

That creates unrealized potential due to the downward bias. An upswing above $1.18 “can quickly make short-inventory assumptions outdated”, states the report. This aligns with the AI-band top by Bitrue’s artificial intelligence (AI) based assistant. This empowers a look at XRP’s price structure without sentimental bias.

The report sets out three signals to watch: volume on up days overtaking volume on down days, on-balance volume turning higher & a daily close above $1.18. In August 2026, the exact shift in volume arrived on the same day as XRP’s price rebound, which is why Bitrue Research Institute’s study recommends watching these signals together.

The outlined three-month setup works like a swiss clock: with the 14-day RSI below chasing down levels below 35, that’s usually bullish for XRP. The study showed 18 events, including 17 with a complete 90-day follow-up window, when XRP finished higher after 90 days in 70.6% of those cases, exceeding the 53.5% in positive-return frequency.

Wallets holding at least 1 million XRP have also climbed to a 2026 high. However, the smaller time-frames showcased a divided sentiment between the top-tier investors. The Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) dipped below zero, flashing from -0.09 to -0.17 on one-hour & four-hour XRP price charts, respectively. That exposes the majority of whales selling XRP.

That kind of crypto whale sentiment has lowered the entrance to the XRP rich-list, as previously reported by DailyCoin. The altcoin’s now trading well below the SuperTrend price range between $1.27 to $1.30, while the research concluded that a median dip of 21% typically preceded a broader rebound rally.

With that structure not being immediate, a test of $1 isn’t far-fetched.

We're still waiting for Team Cherry to say something, anything at all

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Hollow Knight: Silksong has a new physical edition, and early arrivals reveal a surprise message from developer Team Cherry regarding additional content coming to the game in the months and, seemingly, years ahead.

Metroidvania marketing man Matthew Griffin reminds fans that "Hollow Knight: Silksong (Standard Edition) is arriving soon on November 10th." It comes with a user manual, a hand-drawn map, and reversible cover art. We're most interested in that user manual, which ends with a section on "Content Packs," per images shared online by fans who got their physical copy a smidge early.

"The world of Hollow Knight: Silksong will expand with free content packs," this page of the manual reads. "Prepare for even more adventures filled with new friends, new abilities, and new ferocious foes!"

The plurality of this promise is what's raised some eyebrows. After the many DLCs Hollow Knight received, there was little doubt that Team Cherry would add a truckload of content to Silksong. This is not a studio known for restraint. But how would all those ideas be expressed? Official word of several "free content packs" containing multiple "new abilities" for protagonist Hornet has predictably excited fans.

The manual continues: "In the first giant expansion, voyage across and beneath a salt-stricken ocean brimming with mystery and menace." Here again, fans linger on this being the first giant expansion for Silksong.

Hollow Knight also got multiple DLC packs – Hidden Dreams, The Grimm Troupe, Lifeblood, and Godmaster – adding bosses, items, areas, storylines, and exacting arena challenges. Previous comments from developers Ari Gibson and William Pellen suggested Silksong may receive a similar variety of additional content, with Sea of Sorrow expected to be a Grimm Troupe-like shotgun blast of experiences.

The manual concludes with a Hollow Knight: Silksong - Sea of Sorrow emblem that reads, "Coming soon." You'll note that this, like everything else Team Cherry has said, doesn't say when soon is.

Sign up to our Switch 2 Spotlight newsletter to make sure you get the very latest news on Nintendo's new console and its games every week

Last year, Team Cherry announced Sea of Sorrow and charted a 2026 launch. Apart from a February update on a port for the original Hollow Knight and a March post confirming expanded Silksong language support, the studio hasn't said anything else on its blog or social feeds all year. Pellen previously said Team Cherry would be more communicative, and we can see how that panned out. Three blog posts in a year, dare we dream?

In a read that's only slightly generous, it's possible that this blank phrasing, used in place of a definitive release date, is just generalist wording or an artifact of this physical edition's production. Maybe Team Cherry didn't want to announce anything major in such a limited product. But this does read like a step back from the 2026 target in the expansion's reveal trailer.

Team Cherry is quiet and spontaneous enough that a Sea of Sorrow shadow drop, or at least a release date with very little notice, wouldn't surprise me. Silksong's launch also came out of nowhere, giving countless other games just two weeks to flee the blast radius. But 2026 is rapidly coming to a close and we haven't heard a peep from Team Cherry about the expansion, so I also wouldn't be surprised if this manual does, as players fear, herald a delay into 2027.

Recent YouTube comments on Sea of Sorrow's trailer tell the whole story. "Team Cherry...? Where are you...?" wonders one viewer.

The vibes are very similar to those around The Duskbloods, a Nintendo Switch 2-exclusive FromSoftware game long said to be releasing in 2026. As the months flew by without any official word, a delay seemed more and more likely, though FromSoftware also notably had some negative test feedback to respond to. Lo and behold, The Duskbloods was delayed. But much like FromSoftware, Team Cherry can pretty much do what it wants, when it wants, and millions of fans will show up anyway.
